3192 AE9C C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3193 AE9E 0450  20 	b *r0
3194               	;
3195               	; 	' THE FONT'S COLOUR TABLE WAS 472 BYTES OF ONE REPEATED VALUE, which is a
3196               	; 	' fifth of what the fixed area had left. DEFINE COLOR copies such a table
3197               	; 	' out of ROM into all three screen thirds; writing the constant straight
3198               	; 	' into the colour table does the same job for the price of a loop, and
3199               	; 	' the loop runs once, at boot.
3200               	; 	'
3201               	; 	' The colour table is at >2000 and the VDP mirrors it once per screen
3202               	; 	' third, >800 apart. Both numbers were read out of the GENERATED assembly
3203               	; 	' -- define_color's `ai r0,>2000` and LDIRVM3's `ai r0,>0800` -- rather
3204               	; 	' than assumed, because a wrong base here would paint over the pattern
3205               	; 	' table and the failure would look like corrupt artwork.
3206               	; 	'
3207               	; 	' Paced: a few hundred VDP writes in one frame are silently dropped.
3208               	; 	' THE SCANNER'S COLOURS ARE THREE BLOCKS REPEATED SIXTEEN TIMES EACH, so
3209               	; 	' the table shipped 384 bytes to say 24 bytes' worth. Same trick as the
3210               	; 	' font: write the colour table directly. >2000 is the colour table's base
3211               	; 	' and >800 the stride between screen thirds -- the same layout DEFINE COLOR
3212               	; 	' walks for us in setup_font, which this cannot use because it is cheaper to
3213               	; 	' expand 24 bytes here than to ship 384 in the bank.
3214               	; 	' NOTHING IS UNDER THE GROUND FLOOR, SO NOTHING SHOULD BE GREEN THERE.
3215               	; 	' A floor bar is five pixels of yellow over three of the green air
3216               	; 	' belonging to the floor BELOW it (see SLAB), which is right for three of
3217               	; 	' the four bars and wrong for the last one: below floor 0 there is no
3218               	; 	' floor, only the scanner, and the green read as a strip of shop with
3219               	; 	' nothing in it.
3220               	; 	'
3221               	; 	' NO NEW CHARACTER IS NEEDED, BECAUSE THE COLOUR TABLE HAS THREE COPIES
3222               	; 	' -- one per eight screen rows -- and the bands land such that each bar
3223               	; 	' sits in a different third:
3224               	; 	'
3225               	; 	'     band 0 roof   rows  1-5    bar row  5   third 0
3226               	; 	'     band 1        rows  6-10   bar row 10   third 1
3227               	; 	'     band 2        rows 11-15   bar row 15   third 1
3228               	; 	'     band 3 GROUND rows 16-20   bar row 20   third 2
3229               	; 	'
3230               	; 	' so recolouring SLAB in third 2 alone reaches the ground floor's bar and
3231               	; 	' nothing else. The same third also holds the scanner, but the scanner
3232               	; 	' does not use these characters. This is the same mechanism that once hid
3233               	; 	' a bug for months (CLAUDE.md 3A, the blanked-in-one-third note) used
3234               	; 	' deliberately for once.
3235               	; 	'
3236               	; 	' Only the three bottom lines change, and only their BACKGROUND: SLAB has
3237               	; 	' no ink there and SLABE's brick keeps its grey.
3238               	; floor0_colour:
3239               cvb_FLOOR0_COLOUR
3240               	; 	' TWO CHARACTERS, THE SAME THREE SCAN LINES, ONE ROUTINE. Both blocks
3241               	; 	' were written out in full and differed only in which character and which
3242               	; 	' colour byte -- eleven statements twice, including the multiply-by-eight
3243               	; 	' done by doubling (`*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, which clobbers r0,
3244               	; 	' CLAUDE.md 3A).
3245               	; 	f0c = CH_SLAB

3631 B294 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3632 B296 0450  20 	b *r0
3633               	;
3634               	; 	' ------------------------------------------- ALPHA LOCK, CALIBRATED
3635               	; 	' On the TI, ALPHA LOCK shares a line with the joystick's VERTICAL axis.
3636               	; 	' Latched down it reports a direction that is NEVER RELEASED. Every other
3637               	; 	' game in this repo dodges this by not reading up/down at all; this one
3638               	; 	' cannot, because down is the duck and up is the elevator.
3639               	; 	'
3640               	; 	' THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS REFUSED TO START UNTIL THE AXIS CLEARED, AND
3641               	; 	' THAT WAS THE WRONG CALL. Classic99 defaults to invertcaps=1, so the TI
3642               	; 	' sees ALPHA LOCK DOWN when the host's Caps Lock is UP -- the normal
3643               	; 	' state. The game then sat on its title screen for ever waiting for a
3644               	; 	' condition the player had no reason to suspect, which presents as "the
3645               	; 	' title comes up and it will not start". A check that turns a survivable
3646               	; 	' input quirk into a dead game is worse than no check.
3647               	; 	'
3648               	; 	' So: sample the axis for 40 frames BEFORE any input can reasonably have
3649               	; 	' been given. A direction held for essentially all of them is not a player
3650               	; 	' -- it is the key. Record it, say so, and then IGNORE that direction for
3651               	; 	' the whole game, which makes ALPHA LOCK harmless instead of fatal.
3652               	; alock_cal:
3653               cvb_ALOCK_CAL
3654               	; 	alku = 0
3655 B298 04C0  14 	clr r0
3656 B29A D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ALKU
     B29C 2173

3762               	; 	' ALPHA LOCK sample all run AFTER it. For about a second the title is up,
3763               	; 	' finished, and completely deaf. A player types 8-3-8 into that window and
3764               	; 	' the first digit lands in nothing -- then a following 3-8 completes the
3765               	; 	' code, because the 8 they typed second is still standing as state. That
3766               	; 	' is exactly how it was reported, twice, and it is not noise: it is the
3767               	; 	' screen lying about being ready.

3769               	; 	' RallyX and Bust-A-Bobble never showed this because they draw their
3770               	; 	' titles when they are already listening. Keystone cannot -- the whole
3771               	; 	' point of the early draw is that it is early -- so the PROMPT waits
3772               	; 	' instead, and its arrival is the cue that the screen is awake.

     B496 B3BA     
3903               	;
3904               	; 	' 8-3-8 IS EDGE-TRIGGERED AND ANY STRAY DIGIT RESETS IT (see title_wait).
3905               	; 	'
3906               	; 	' It used to read cont1.key raw every pass and test only for the digit it
3907               	; 	' wanted next. That worked -- but only because 8-3-8 ALTERNATES, so holding
3908               	; 	' 8 cannot advance past the first step. It is an accident of the sequence
3909               	; 	' rather than a design, and it had two costs: 8,5,3,9,8 opened this page as
3910               	; 	' readily as 8,3,8, and a key that READS as held for many frames got a free
3911               	; 	' walk through the state machine.
3912               	; 	'
3913               	; 	' That second one is not hypothetical on this machine. ALPHA LOCK shorts a
3914               	; 	' keyboard line -- and Classic99 defaults to invertcaps, so it reads DOWN
3915               	; 	' with the host's Caps Lock UP -- and this page was reached twice from
3916               	; 	' single keypresses while testing an unrelated change.
3917               	; 	'
3918               	; 	' The reset is the DEFAULT rather than a test for a particular wrong digit:
3919               	; 	' Bust-A-Bobble resets only on a stray 3 and still lets 8,5,3,8 through.
3920               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------- 838 setup page
3921               	; setup838:
3922               cvb_SETUP838
3923               	; 	CLS
3924 B498 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B49A A980     
3925 B49C A0FC     	data cls
3926               	; 	' ONE PROMPT AT A TIME, NOTHING ELSE ON THE SCREEN, AND NO NUMBERS UNTIL
3927               	; 	' THEY ARE TYPED. There is no heading and no instructions: a page showing
3928               	; 	' exactly one question does not need to explain that one digit answers it,
3929               	; 	' and the second question does not exist until the first is answered.
3930               	; 	'
3931               	; 	' Showing the CURRENT values first was the obvious thing and it cost more
3932               	; 	' than it gave -- two draw routines, a "fire keeps what is shown" escape to
3933               	; 	' make the display mean something, and the defaults had to be applied here
3934               	; 	' as well as in new_game so the page had numbers to show at all. None of it
3935               	; 	' survives: every digit on screen is one the player just typed. Four
3936               	; 	' strings became two, a two-field loop became a straight line, and the page

4263               	; 	' pinned rather than placed. 120 is the middle of the walkable range
4264               	; 	' (XWALW 8 to XWALL 232), so he starts with room on both sides and the
4265               	; 	' first thing he does is a choice rather than the only move available.

4297               	; 	' never reset when play began, so it carried its value in from whatever
4298               	; 	' happened last. Press FIRE on the title with jrel left at 1 and Kelly
4299               	; 	' jumps on the first frame of the round, having been told to do so by the
4300               	; 	' keypress that only meant "start".

4302               	; 	' It hid on the very first game of a session, because CVBasic zeroes its
4303               	; 	' variables and jrel = 0 already means "wait for a release" -- so it only
4304               	; 	' appeared on the SECOND round onwards, which reads as intermittent.

4330               	; 	' From the lift on screen 3 that is 5,120 px against the 4,208 he walked

4332               	; 	' round: he could never escape at all, and one of the two loss conditions
4333               	; 	' would quietly have stopped existing. This block used to say exactly that

4335               	; 	'

4337               	; 	' per pass is worth about TEN SECONDS of his route, so the two candidate
4338               	; 	' values straddled the buzzer with nothing in between:
4339               	; 	'
4340               	; 	'   2.00 px/pass   escapes at 102.4 s of a 100 s round   never escapes
4341               	; 	'   2.25 px/pass   escapes at  91.0 s                    nine seconds early
4342               	; 	'
4343               	; 	' Worse, 2.25 was not reachable at all: the accumulator below had two

4345               	; 	' silently delivered 2.00 and he fell two thirds of a screen short of his
4346               	; 	' own escape. See the accumulator comment for the full account.
4347               	; 	'
4348               	; 	' Sixteenths with three drains put the step at about 2.5 s instead of 10,
4349               	; 	' and hsp64 = 35 (2.1875 px/pass) lands him at 96.3 s of the 100 --

4356               	; 	' Everything that moves is paced per loop PASS while the clock is paced in

4540               	; 	' spawns beside floor 2's escalator and so skips a traverse. A chase

4542               	; 	' Harry's speed before he is ahead at all. See DESIGN.md 4a.

4546               	; 	' widths a second and its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At 1.5 ours
4547               	; 	' was 2.67 and the crook visibly strolled. 1.75 brings it to 2.29.

4552               	; 	' than one beach ball (9 s). At 1.75 he is 10.1 s ahead, which absorbs a

4586 B8FA C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4587 B8FC 0450  20 	b *r0
4588               	;
4589               	; 	' ======================================================================
4590               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4591               	; 	' ======================================================================
4592               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4593               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4594               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4595               	; 	' of running.
4596               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4597               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4598               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4599               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4600               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4601               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4602               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4603               	; 	'
4604               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4605               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4606               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4607               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4608               	; 	'
4609               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4610               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4611               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4612               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4613               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4614               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4615               	; 	'
4616               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4617               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4618               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4619               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4620               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4621               	; 	'
4622               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4623               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4624               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4625               	; draw_screen:
4626               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4627               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4628 B8FE 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B900 A980     
4629 B902 EADE     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY
4630               	; 	GOSUB load_band
4631 B904 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B906 A980     
4632 B908 BCF2     	data cvb_LOAD_BAND
4633               	; 	FOR dq = 0 TO 3
4634 B90A 04C0  14 	clr r0
4635 B90C D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_DQ
     B90E 21B5

4637               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4638               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4639               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4640               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4641               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4642               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4643               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4644               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4645               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4646               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.

4733 B9E8 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0

4735               	;
4736               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4737               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4738               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4739               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4740               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4741               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4742               	; 	'
4743               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4744               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4745               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4746               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4747               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4748               	; 	'
4749               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4750               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4751               	; 	' above it at all.
4752               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4753               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4754               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4755               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4756               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4757               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4758               	; 	'
4759               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4760               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4761               	; beam_one:
4762               cvb_BEAM_ONE
4763               	; 	IF bt > 2 THEN RETURN		' the roof has no band above it

5296               	; 			' AND HOW MANY FLOORS CARRY ONE AT ALL. The gates above
5297               	; 			' decide what KIND a hazard is; this decides whether the
5298               	; 			' floor has one.
5299               	; 			'
5300               	; 			' The kind-arrival rule downgrades anything that has not
5301               	; 			' arrived yet to a beach ball rather than leaving the floor
5302               	; 			' empty, so that a floor is never bare -- and the effect on
5303               	; 			' Krook 1 was a ball on all FOUR floors of every populated
5304               	; 			' screen. All four bands are on screen at once, so that is
5305               	; 			' four identical balls in view at all times, which is not
5306               	; 			' what "merely a few beach balls" describes.
5307               	; 			'
5308               	; 			' Per-band count was never the problem: only one slot is
5309               	; 			' live until Krook 6. It is the number of OCCUPIED FLOORS,
5310               	; 			' and it had no ramp at all.
5311               	; 			'
5312               	; 			' Floors 1 and 3 stay clear on Krook 1, floor 1 joins on
5313               	; 			' Krook 2, and the roof on Krook 3. ALTERNATING rather than
5314               	; 			' clearing the top or bottom half, so the empty floors do
5315               	; 			' not stack into a visibly dead region that reads as a bug
5316               	; 			' -- and the ROOF is last, because that is where the round
5317               	; 			' is decided.
5318               	; 			IF krk < 2 THEN

5356               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
5357               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
5358               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
5359               	; 			'
5360               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
5361               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
5362               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
5363               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
5364               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
5365               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
5366               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.

5369               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be

5373               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs
5374               	; 			' past is not what this rule is about.

5407               	; 			' A RADIO NEEDS A CELL, NOT A PIXEL, because it is drawn as
5408               	; 			' characters. Divided by repeated subtraction: `/` compiles
5409               	; 			' to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A), and this runs once
5410               	; 			' per screen where the divide would run per obstacle -- so
5411               	; 			' the loop is the cheaper of the two, and the only one that
5412               	; 			' cannot be got wrong by a rounding rule.
5413               	; 			obc(li) = 0
5414 BECC 04C1  14 	clr r1

     BED0 21DE

     BED6 2272     
5418 BED8 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5419               	; 			' PER KIND, not one global speed (see start_krook).
5420               	; 			' THE SPEED IS A PROPERTY OF THE KIND, and obs() only ever
5421               	; 			' held a copy of it -- set from the kind here, zeroed for a
5422               	; 			' radio below, and read in one place. upd_obst derives it
5423               	; 			' from obk() instead, which is 8 bytes of RAM back and one
5424               	; 			' fewer thing that can disagree with itself.
5425               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
5426               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5427               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5428               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5429               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5430               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.
5431               	; 			'
5432               	; 			' The table's x becomes a stagger from that edge rather than
5433               	; 			' an absolute position, and the slot index spreads them out
5434               	; 			' so they arrive in sequence instead of as one wall.
5435               	; 			'
5436               	; 			' THE SECOND ONE IS A FIXED DISTANCE BEHIND THE FIRST, AND
5437               	; 			' THAT DISTANCE IS SET BY THE JUMP.
5438               	; 			'
5439               	; 			' It used to be `(lx AND 63) + 64` -- a masked byte out of
5440               	; 			' the placement table plus a constant -- so the gap between
5441               	; 			' the two hazards was whatever those two bytes happened to
5442               	; 			' differ by. Measured across the five populated screens it
5443               	; 			' came out 46, 46, 70, 70, 70 px. Screens 2 and 4 flank the
5444               	; 			' elevator, so the store had a visibly tighter pair on one
5445               	; 			' side of it than the other, which is exactly how it was
5446               	; 			' reported from play.
5447               	; 			'
5448               	; 			' Both numbers are too small, which is the real fault. Kelly
5449               	; 			' closes on an oncoming hazard at WALKSP + the hazard's own
5450               	; 			' speed = 6 px a pass, and a jump lasts about 30 passes, so
5451               	; 			' a jump eats ~120 px of closing distance. At 46 or 70 there
5452               	; 			' is NO screen where he can land between the two: he clears
5453               	; 			' the first and comes down on the second.
5454               	; 			'
5455               	; 			' HAZGAP is that distance with margin. The first hazard
5456               	; 			' still lands where the table puts it, so the placement
5457               	; 			' variety is unchanged; only the pairing is now a fact about
5458               	; 			' the jump rather than an accident of two bytes.
5459               	; 			' assets/checkspace.py measures it and fails the build.
5460               	; 			' THE SECOND SLOT IS MEASURED FROM THE FIRST, not from its
5461               	; 			' own table byte. Adding HAZGAP to its own `lx AND 63` would
5462               	; 			' still leave the GAP varying by up to 63 px between screens
5463               	; 			' -- the same fault in a politer form, and the reason the two
5464               	; 			' sides of the elevator differed in the first place. Holding
5465               	; 			' slot 0's stagger and adding to that makes the gap exactly
5466               	; 			' HAZGAP on every screen, while slot 0 still lands wherever
5467               	; 			' the table puts it, so the placement variety is untouched.
5468               	; 			stag = lx AND 63
5469 BEDA D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0

5549               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5550               	; 			'
5551               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5552               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5553               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5554               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5555               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5556               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5557               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5558               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5559               	; 			'
5560               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5561               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5562               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5563               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5564               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5565               	; 			'
5566               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5567               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5568               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5569               	; 			'
5570               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5571               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5572               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5573               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5574               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5575               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5576               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31

6226               	; 	' ONE CELL OF THE DOORWAY, from its column (ccl 0-3), its band row
6227               	; 	' (crw 0-3) and the door state (cst). Four columns and four rows, so

6229               	; 	' edges: column 0 and column 3 carry the JAMB, row 0 carries the LINTEL
6230               	; 	' and row 3 the SILL.
6231               	; 	'
6232               	; 	' THE SILL IS REVEALED AS THE DOORS PART, which falls out of this rather
6233               	; 	' than being arranged: shut, every cell is plain door and there is no
6234               	; 	' sill to see; part-open, only the middle two columns show car and sill;
6235               	; 	' open, all four do. A sill sitting under a shut door would be a ledge
6236               	; 	' with nothing behind it.

6466               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6467               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6468               	; 		'
6469               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6470               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6471               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6472               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6473               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6474               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6475               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6476               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6477               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6478               	; 		'
6479               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6480               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6481               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6482               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6483               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6484               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1

6787               	; 	' --- running
6788               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
6789               	; 	'
6790               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6791               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6792               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6793               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6794               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6795               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
6796               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
6797               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was
6798               	; 	' facing halfway through an arc. kjdx is 0 for a standing jump, 1 for a
6799               	; 	' running one to the right, 2 to the left.

7008               cv379
7009               	; 		' THE FIRST STEP OFF THE MARK IS ALWAYS HEARD. sfw is a distance --
7010               	; 		' a step every fifteen pixels -- so a short tap of the stick moved
7011               	; 		' him a few pixels and made NO sound at all. That does not read as
7012               	; 		' a short step, it reads as the controls being ignored, and it is
7013               	; 		' worst exactly where a player taps rather than holds: lining up a
7014               	; 		' jump, or edging around a hazard.
7015               	; 		'
7016               	; 		' Priming the accumulator to the threshold makes the very next pass
7017               	; 		' fire. It does not cheat the RATE: the trigger subtracts fifteen
7018               	; 		' rather than zeroing, so the pixels actually travelled still carry
7019               	; 		' into the following step and a held run comes out at 6.9 a second
7020               	; 		' either way.
7021               	; 		IF kmvl = 0 THEN sfw = 15
7022 CAE6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KMVL,r0
     CAE8 2207     
7023 CAEA 1302  14 	jeq cv381
7024 CAEC 0460  28 	b @cv380
     CAEE CAF8     
7025               cv381
7026 CAF0 0200  20 	li r0,3840   ; 15*256
     CAF2 0F00     
7027 CAF4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_SFW
     CAF6 220A     
7028               cv380
7029               	; 		' AN ODOMETER, NOT A CLOCK -- pixels travelled, exactly like
7030               	; 		' Harry's hanim. It counted PASSES once, which made his legs cycle
7031               	; 		' slower on the escalator screens while he still ran at the same
7032               	; 		' speed; that was fixed by counting the frame DELTA instead, which
7033               	; 		' traded one mismatch for another -- his legs then kept wall-clock
7034               	; 		' time while his body still moved once per pass, so his stride
7035               	; 		' slipped against the ground whenever the loop was busy.
7036               	; 		'
7037               	; 		' Distance settles it. A pose per 8 px at any frame rate, and a
7038               	; 		' footstep is a stride rather than a tick.
7039               	; 		kanim = kanim + kspd
7040 CAF8 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KANIM,r0
     CAFA 20FF

7153               	;
7154               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
7155               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
7156               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
7157               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
7158               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
7159               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
7160               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
7161               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
7162               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
7163               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
7164               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
7165               	; 	'
7166               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
7167               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
7168               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
7169               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
7170               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

7207               	; 	' own x. A step's centre sits at 8 x its index, so the bottom step (9)

7729               	;
7730               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7731               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7732               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7733               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7734               	; 	'
7735               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7736               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7737               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7738               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7739               	; 	'
7740               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7741               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7742               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7743               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7744               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7745               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7746               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7747               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7748               	; 	'
7749               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7750               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7751               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7752               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7753               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7754               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7755               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7756               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7757               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7758               	; 	'
7759               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7760               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7761               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7762               	; wipe_shelf:
7763               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7764               	; 	wsn = 8

7846               	; 	' nothing.

7913               	; 	' on the roof it is the SKYLINE. Painting green over either punched a
7914               	; 	' two-row hole through the building -- and because the band is blitted and
7915               	; 	' then corrected within the same pass, you could watch it happen at round

8402               	; upd_obst:
8403               cvb_UPD_OBST
8404               	; 	' ONE ACCUMULATOR PER KIND, NOT PER SLOT. Every hazard of a kind moves at
8405               	; 	' that kind's speed, so three calls a pass serve all eight slots.
8406               	; 	'
8407               	; 	' Per-slot would have meant up to eight calls of O(fdv) work every pass --
8408               	; 	' the positive feedback loop CLAUDE.md 3A warns about, where a slow pass is
8409               	; 	' made slower by the very code meant to take the loop rate out of the
8410               	; 	' game's behaviour. It also saves the eight bytes an obacc() would cost,
8411               	; 	' and ColecoVision RAM is the tighter budget.
8412               	; 	pacc = oacb

8445               	; 	' THE BOUNCE PHASE, ON THE SAME CLOCK AS THE JUMP AND AT ITS OLD PERIOD.
8446               	; 	'
8447               	; 	' The jump has always advanced by the frame delta (`kjf = kjf + fdv`) while
8448               	; 	' the bounce advanced once per PASS -- so whether a jump cleared a ball
8449               	; 	' depended on how busy the screen was, and the frame-by-frame property
8450               	; 	' checkball.py proves was exact only at fdv = 1.
8451               	; 	'
8452               	; 	' Stepping the phase by fdv directly would fix the clock and wreck the
8453               	; 	' game: a 32-phase arc would drop from about 1.35 s to 0.55 s, balls
8454               	; 	' bouncing 2.4x faster everywhere. So it gets a fraction of its own --
8455               	; 	' 25/64 of a phase per frame is 23.4 phases a second, against the 23.8 it
8456               	; 	' runs at today. Same bounce, now the same clock as the thing that has to
8457               	; 	' clear it.

8673 D712 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8674 D714 0450  20 	b *r0
8675               	;
8676               	; 	' ONE STEP OF A FRACTIONAL SPEED, FOR WHOEVER IS MOVING.
8677               	; 	'
8678               	; 	' `pacc` in and out, `psp64` in (sixty-fourths of a pixel per FRAME),
8679               	; 	' `pspd` out (whole pixels this pass). CVBasic has no locals, so the caller
8680               	; 	' stages its own accumulator in and takes it back out; that costs four
8681               	; 	' statements against the fourteen this used to be, written out per actor.
8682               	; 	'
8683               	; 	' Harry had the only copy. Kelly, the hazards and the bounce phase all need
8684               	; 	' the same thing -- everything the round clock judges has to advance by
8685               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAMES, or the loop rate becomes a difficulty dial and where the
8686               	; 	' player stands decides the outcome (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
8687               	; 	'
8688               	; 	' TWO DRAINS CAP THE STEP AT 2 PER FRAME whatever psp64 says (CLAUDE.md
8689               	; 	' 3A: N drains cap the speed at N, and the surplus leaks into a byte that
8690               	; 	' wraps). Every caller is under that: Kelly 102/64 = 1.6, the fastest
8691               	; 	' hazard 76/64 = 1.2, Harry 57/64 = 0.9, the bounce phase 25/64 = 0.4.
8692               	; 	'
8693               	; 	' FIVE FRAMES, AND THREE WAS TOO FEW. At 2 px a frame five frames is 10 px,
8694               	; 	' and to skip a position window an actor must start outside it on one side
8695               	; 	' and land outside on the other, which needs 12. Three was the original bug
8696               	; 	' in miniature: the lift screen runs at almost exactly 20 passes a second,
8697               	; 	' which IS fdv = 3, so every hitch there lost a frame. The clamp exists for
8698               	; 	' a genuine stall and at five it only fires for one.
8699               	; pace_step:
8700               cvb_PACE_STEP
8701               	; 	pfd = fdv
8702 D716 D820  54 	movb @cvb_FDV,@cvb_PFD
     D718 212C

9185               	;
9186               	; 	' THE SIXTEENTH-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen
9187               	; 	' (2), so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here
9188               	; 	' and is spent as whole pixels. THREE IFs, not a loop and not a divide:
9189               	; 	' `%` and `/` both compile to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this
9190               	; 	' runs every pass.
9191               	; 	'
9192               	; 	' THE NUMBER OF DRAINS IS A CEILING ON THE SPEED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE
9193               	; 	' POINT OF THIS COMMENT. Each IF can spend one whole pixel, so N drains
9194               	; 	' cap hspd at N px per pass NO MATTER WHAT hsp64 SAYS. The invariant is:
9195               	; 	'
9196               	; 	'     hacc is under 16 on entry, and hsp64 is at most 48,
9197               	; 	'     so hacc is under 64 here and three drains always suffice.
9198               	; 	'
9199               	; 	' KEEP hsp64 <= 48. This was quarter-pixels with TWO drains and the same
9200               	; 	' invariant written the same way -- "hsp4 is at most 8, so it can never
9201               	; 	' need a third" -- and it was correct until somebody set hsp4 = 9 without
9202               	; 	' re-reading it. The crook then ran at a flat 2.0 px/pass while every
9203               	; 	' comment, the design table and assets/checkchase.py all said 2.25, and
9204               	; 	' the surplus quarter leaked into hacc and wrapped the byte every 256
9205               	; 	' passes. Nothing failed; he simply arrived two thirds of a screen short
9206               	; 	' of his own escape. checkchase.py now counts these IFs and refuses a
9207               	; 	' speed the drains cannot deliver.
9208               	; 	'
9209               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
9210               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
9211               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
9212               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
9213               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
9214               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
9215               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
9216               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
9217               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
9218               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
9219               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
9220               	; 	' flight looking busy.
9221               	; 	'
9222               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
9223               	; 	'
9224               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
9225               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
9226               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
9227               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
9228               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
9229               	; 	'
9230               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
9231               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
9232               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
9233               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
9234               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN

9316               	;
9317               	; 	' HE WALKS BY REAL TIME, NOT BY LOOP PASS -- one accumulate-and-drain per
9318               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAME rather than per pass. Everything that moves here used to be
9319               	; 	' per pass while the CLOCK is paced by the frame delta, so a busy screen
9320               	; 	' slowed Harry in real seconds and the clock did not slow with him: the
9321               	; 	' same uninterrupted escape finished with nine timer units in hand from a
9322               	; 	' light screen and none at all from the lift screen, decided by where the
9323               	; 	' player happened to be standing. Now he covers the same ground per second
9324               	; 	' wherever anybody is (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
9325               	; 	'
9326               	; 	' A LOOP, NOT A MULTIPLY. `*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, and reading a
9327               	; 	' 16-bit variable straight after one returns the product's HIGH word
9328               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A). fdv is 1..3 here, so this is at most three adds.
9329               	; 	'
9330               	; 	' SIXTY-FOURTHS, AND ONLY TWO DRAINS -- both fall out of accumulating per
9331               	; 	' FRAME instead of per pass. He covers under a pixel a frame, so two drains
9332               	; 	' are ample where the per-pass version needed three; and the finer unit is
9333               	; 	' what makes the speed tunable at all. In sixteenths one step was about six
9334               	; 	' SECONDS of his route, so nothing landed near the buzzer; in sixty-fourths
9335               	; 	' it is about one and a half. Keep hsp64 <= 128: hacc is under 64 on entry,
9336               	; 	' so 64 + 128 stays inside a byte and two drains always suffice.
9337               	; 	'
9338               	; 	' AND THE STEP IS CLAMPED TO THREE FRAMES' WORTH, WHICH IS NOT A DETAIL.
9339               	; 	' Two tests sample his position once a pass and neither interpolates:
9340               	; 	'
9341               	; 	'   ARRIVAL   `hdx < 6` around the escalator or the roof door -- an ELEVEN
9342               	; 	'             pixel window. A step over 10 px can jump clean across it and
9343               	; 	'             he never arrives at all.
9344               	; 	'   THE CATCH `hdd < CATCHR` is 12, a 23-wide band, and Kelly closes 4 of
9345               	; 	'             it himself -- so Harry over ~18 px could pass through the Kop
9346               	; 	'             between two passes without either sample being inside it.
9347               	; 	'
9348               	; 	' The clamp and the drains live in pace_step now, with the reasoning; the
9349               	; 	' numbers above are what sized them. Measured: TIME 02 on the lift screen
9350               	; 	' against TIME 04 on a light one when the clamp was 3, and 00 against 09
9351               	; 	' before any of this.
9352               	; 	'
9353               	; 	' The ESCALATOR RIDE is deliberately untouched: it returns above this,
9354               	; 	' clocked one step per pass to stay locked to the moving staircase. A rider
9355               	; 	' advanced by the frame delta either drifts off the treads or makes the
9356               	; 	' steps run backwards (CLAUDE.md 3A, DESIGN.md 0f). It is about 3% of his
9357               	; 	' journey.
9358               	; 	'
9359               	; 	' `hanim` still advances by hspd and is still an ODOMETER -- pixels
9360               	; 	' travelled, not time -- so the run cycle stays locked to the ground at any
9361               	; 	' rate. A 9 px step moves it exactly one pose of its 8 px beat.
9362               	; 	pacc = hacc

9527               	; 	' hspd is 0 on a third of his frames. On every one of those the legs took

9793               	;
9794               	; 	' ======================================================================
9795               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
9796               	; 	' ======================================================================
9797               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
9798               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
9799               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
9800               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
9801               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
9802               	; 	'
9803               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
9804               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
9805               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
9806               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
9807               	; 	' broken.
9808               	; 	'
9809               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
9810               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
9811               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
9812               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
9813               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
9814               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
9815               	; 	'
9816               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
9817               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
9818               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
9819               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
9820               	; coll_obst:
9821               cvb_COLL_OBST

10119               	; 	' THE FLOOR CLEARS WHEN YOU ARE HIT, and stays clear until you re-enter
10120               	; 	' the screen. Nine seconds is a heavy penalty on a fifty-unit clock, and
10121               	; 	' taking it while still standing among the things that charged it -- with
10122               	; 	' a second hazard 48 px behind the first -- is how one mistake becomes
10123               	; 	' three. Clearing the row makes the penalty a single event you can walk
10124               	; 	' away from.
10125               	; 	'
10126               	; 	' BOTH SLOTS, WHATEVER IS IN THEM. Radios used to be exempt: they are
10127               	; 	' CHARACTERS stamped into the name table rather than sprites, so zeroing
10128               	; 	' the kind would have stopped them colliding while leaving them plainly
10129               	; 	' visible on the shelf, and a fixture that is still there has to still be
10130               	; 	' there. That was a reason to UNDRAW them, not a reason to keep them --
10131               	; 	' and it left the one hazard that cannot leave on its own as the only one
10132               	; 	' the mercy did not cover. haz_off now takes the characters with it.
10133               	; 	'
10134               	; 	' NOTHING RESTORES IT HERE. load_band repopulates the band from the
10135               	; 	' template, and it runs only on a seam crossing or a round start, so
10136               	; 	' "until the screen is re-entered" costs no state and no timer: leave and
10137               	; 	' come back and the hazards are simply placed again.
10138               	; 	GOSUB haz_gone
10139 E15E 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     E160 A980     
10140 E162 D358     	data cvb_HAZ_GONE
10141               	; 	' A HIT DOES NOT END A JUMP. This used to force ST_RUN and zero the arc,
10142               	; 	' which dropped him straight down out of mid-air onto whatever he happened
10143               	; 	' to be over -- and since the arc is ballistic and ignores the stick once
10144               	; 	' he is airborne (0l), that read as the game taking the controls away
10145               	; 	' rather than as a hit landing. The penalty is the nine units and the
10146               	; 	' knock flash; where he comes down is still his own arc.
10147               	; 	'
10148               	; 	' A ducking Kop is still stood up, because the crouch is a held pose and
10149               	; 	' there is nothing to interrupt.
10150               	; 	IF klst <> ST_JUMP THEN
10151 E164 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     E166 21B0

     E16A 0100

10308               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both
10309               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the
10310               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
10311               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
10312               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
10313               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
10314               	; 	'
10315               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
10316               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
10317               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
10318               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.

10351               	; draw_actors:
10352               cvb_DRAW_ACTORS
10353               	; 	' RIDING WITH THE DOORS SHUT: HIDE HIM AND DO NOT DRAW HIM AT ALL.
10354               	; 	'
10355               	; 	' This test used to sit AFTER his sprites had been written, so every pass
10356               	; 	' placed him at his old floor and then hid him again. That is not free.
10357               	; 	' CVBasic's SPRITE writes a RAM mirror which the vblank ISR copies to
10358               	; 	' VRAM, so a vblank landing BETWEEN the draw and the hide latches the
10359               	; 	' visible state for a frame -- and draw_actors is long (Kelly, Harry,
10360               	; 	' eight obstacles) while a pass on the lift screen spans two or three
10361               	; 	' frames, so it happened constantly. He flickered at the floor he had
10362               	; 	' just left, which reads as a drawing bug rather than as a journey.
10363               	; 	'
10364               	; 	' Deciding BEFORE drawing costs one test and cannot race the ISR at all:
10365               	; 	' the mirror never holds a position to be caught with.
10366               	; 	IF klst = ST_ELEV THEN
10367 E2FE D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     E300 21B0

10444               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
10445               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
10446               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
10447               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
10448               	; 	'
10449               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
10450               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
10451               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
10452               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
10453               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
10454               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
10455               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

10600               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
10601               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
10602               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
10603               	; 		'
10604               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
10605               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
10606               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
10607               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
10608               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.
10609               	; 		kq = P_KLEG1
10610 E4E0 0200  20 	li r0,14336   ; 56*256
     E4E2 3800     
10611 E4E4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_KQ
     E4E6 21DA     
10612               	; 		' BITS 3 AND 4, NOT 2 AND 3 -- A POSE EVERY 8 PIXELS, which is
10613               	; 		' Harry's beat (hanim AND 8 / AND 16) and now means the same thing,
10614               	; 		' because both counters hold pixels travelled. On the old 4-count
10615               	; 		' beat an odometer would have flipped his legs every single pass at
10616               	; 		' 4 px a pass, which is a blur rather than a run. checkanim reads
10617               	; 		' these bits out of the source, and fails if two bands of one figure
10618               	; 		' run on different ones -- so the body below moves with them.

10803               	; 	' BITS 3 AND 4: one pose per 8 px of travel, a 32 px four-beat cycle.
10804               	; 	' `hanim` advances by `hspd`, which is PIXELS MOVED THIS PASS, so a pose
10805               	; 	' is a DISTANCE and not a duration -- it cannot drift with the frame rate
10806               	; 	' or scrub his feet at any speed.
10807               	; 	'
10808               	; 	' HALVING THIS WAS TRIED AND IS WORSE. Bits 4 and 5 put a pose on 16 px
10809               	; 	' and the cycle on 64, which is closer to a real stride for a figure 24 px
10810               	; 	' tall and was rejected on sight. Do not re-derive it from his height; the
10811               	; 	' faster cadence is what the reference reads like at this size.
10812               	; 	IF hanim AND 8 THEN hq = hq + 4

10836               	; 	' THE LEG STRIPES AND THE SHOES ARE THE SAME POSE IN BLACK, and they are
10837               	; 	' derived FROM hq rather than reproducing the two IFs. Two copies of the
10838               	; 	' same phase arithmetic is two clocks, and the day one of them changed
10839               	; 	' the shoes would walk half a stride behind the feet.
10840               	; 	hqs = hq - P_HLEG1
10841 E6C6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_HQ,r0

10844               	; 	hqs = hqs + P_HLEGS1
10845 E6D2 0220  22 	ai r0,40960	; 160
     E6D4 A000     
10846 E6D6 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_HQS
     E6D8 21C2     
10847               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
10848               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
10849               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
10850               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
10851               	; 	'
10852               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
10853               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
10854               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
10855               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
10856               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
10857               	; 	'
10858               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
10859               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the

10948               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
10949               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
10950               	; 	'
10951               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
10952               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
10953               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
10954               	; 	' a jump.
10955               	; 	'
10956               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8` -- the
10957               	; 	' cycle's LOW beat bit. IT MUST MOVE WITH THE BEAT BITS ABOVE IT: on the
10958               	; 	' wrong bit he rises and falls twice per stride, a second clock inside one
10959               	; 	' figure, which is the flapping of DESIGN.md 0j2 in the vertical.
10960               	; 	'
10961               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
10962               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
10963               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is

10970               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the

11446 EB62 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11447 EB64 0450  20 	b *r0
11448               	;
11449               	; 	' ======================================================================
11450               	; 	' THE SCANNER
11451               	; 	' ======================================================================
11452               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
11453               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
11454               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
11455               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
11456               	; 	'
11457               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
11458               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
11459               	; 	'
11460               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
11461               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
11462               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
11463               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
11464               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
11465               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
11466               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
11467               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
11468               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
11469               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
11470               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
11471               	; esc_tick:
11472               cvb_ESC_TICK
11473               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
11474               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
11475               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
11476               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11477               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
11478 EB66 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EB68 219C     
11479 EB6A 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     EB6C 0100     
11480 EB6E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11481 EB70 1402  14 	jhe cv806
11482 EB72 0460  28 	b @cv805
     EB74 EB8A     
11483               cv806
11484               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
11485 EB76 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EB78 219C     
11486 EB7A 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     EB7C 0700     
11487 EB7E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11488 EB80 1A02  14 	jl cv808
11489 EB82 0460  28 	b @cv807
     EB84 EB8A     
11490               cv808
11491 EB86 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11492 EB88 0450  20 	b *r0
11493               cv807
11494               	; 	END IF
11495               cv805
11496               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
11497               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
11498               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
11499               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
11500               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
11501               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
11502               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
11503               	; 	'
11504               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
11505               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
11506               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
11507               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
11508               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
11509               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
11510               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
11511               	; 	escp = escp + 1
11512 EB8A D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     EB8C 20B1     
11513 EB8E 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     EB90 0100     
11514 EB92 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EB94 20B1     
11515               	; 	IF escp > 3 THEN escp = 0
11516 EB96 0201  20 	li r1,1024   ; 4*256
     EB98 0400     
11517 EB9A 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11518 EB9C 1402  14 	jhe cv810
11519 EB9E 0460  28 	b @cv809
     EBA0 EBA8     
11520               cv810
11521 EBA2 04C0  14 	clr r0
11522 EBA4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EBA6 20B1     
11523               cv809
11524               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
11525               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
11526               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
11527               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
11528               	; 	' 7 an east one.
11529               	; 	'
11530               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
11531               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
11532               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
11533               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
11534               	; 	'
11535               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
11536               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
11537               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
11538               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
11539               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
11540               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
11541               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
11542               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
11543               	; 	'
11544               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
11545               	; 	' move at all.
11546               	; 	IF klsc = 0 THEN
11547 EBA8 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EBAA 219C     
11548 EBAC 1302  14 	jeq cv812
11549 EBAE 0460  28 	b @cv811
     EBB0 EC38     
11550               cv812
11551               	; 		IF escp = 0 THEN DEFINE CHAR 110,6,esc_phw0
11552 EBB2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     EBB4 20B1     
11553 EBB6 1302  14 	jeq cv814

11806 EDE6 0450  20 	b *r0
11807               	;
11808               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11809               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11810               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11811               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11812               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11813               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11814               	; 	'
11815               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11816               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11817               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11818               	; 	'
11819               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11820               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11821               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11822               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11823               	; 	'
11824               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11825               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11826               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11827               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11828               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11829               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11830               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
11831               	; 	'
11832               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
11833               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
11834               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
11835               	; 	'
11836               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
11837               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
11838               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
11839               	; scan_furn:
11840               cvb_SCAN_FURN

11881               	; 		' THE ESCALATOR DIAGONALS USED TO BE DRAWN HERE TOO, and it was dead
11882               	; 		' work: scan_escs runs immediately below and draws the same three rows
11883               	; 		' for the same three floors. Every input is a pure function of fl --
11884               	; 		' scan_base, the side lookup, the masks, scan_pat -- and scan_or1 is an
11885               	; 		' OR, so doing it twice cannot differ from doing it once. Sixteen
11886               	; 		' statements, about 320 bytes, and nine VDP writes a floor that nothing
11887               	; 		' on screen could reflect.
11888               	; 		'
11889               	; 		' It is the shape a routine takes when a feature is split out and the
11890               	; 		' original is left behind -- scan_escs is this block plus the colour
11891               	; 		' write that was added later, and it carried a copy of the explanatory
11892               	; 		' comment as well as the code.
11893               	; 		'
11894               	; 		' A SCREENSHOT COULD NOT HAVE SETTLED IT: the radar is three pixel rows
11895               	; 		' tall and Classic99 clips the bottom of the screen. It was proved by
11896               	; 		' replaying both versions against a model of the pattern table for all
11897               	; 		' eight escalator-side combinations.
11898               	; 		WAIT

12178 F07A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
12179 F07C 0450  20 	b *r0
12180               	;
12181               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
12182               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
12183               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
12184               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
12185               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
12186               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12187               	; scan_wipe:
12188               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
12189               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
12190               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
12191               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
12192               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
12193               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
12194               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
12195               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
12196               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
12197               	; 	'
12198               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
12199               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
12200               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
12201               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
12202               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
12203               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
12204               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
12205               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
12206               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
12207               	; 	'
12208               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
12209               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
12210               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
12211               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
12212               	; 	'
12213               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
12214               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
12215               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
12216 F07E 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     F080 1680     
12217 F082 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__SWA
     F084 20E2

     F10A 21F6     
12283               	;
12284               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
12285               	; 	'
12286               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
12287               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
12288               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
12289               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
12290               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
12291               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
12292               	; 	'
12293               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
12294               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
12295               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
12296               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
12297               	; 	'
12298               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
12299               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
12300               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
12301               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
12302               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
12303               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
12304               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
12305               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
12306               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
12307               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
12308               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
