13098               	;
13099               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
13100               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
13101               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
13102               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
13103               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
13104               	; 	'
13105               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
13106               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
13107               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
13108               	; 	'
13109               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
13110               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
13111               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
13112               	; 	'
13113               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
13114               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).

13217 F810 0460  28 	b @cvb_BN_LOOP
     F812 F764     
13218               	;
13219               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
13220               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
13221               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
13222               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
13223               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
13224               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
13225               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
13226               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
13227               	; 	'
13228               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
13229               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
13230               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13231               	;
13232               	; do_escape:
13233               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
13234               	; 	escapd = 0
13235 F814 04C0  14 	clr r0

     F818 2114

13244               	; 	' flags are cleared -- clearing first made the biplane test dead code and

13271 F854 EB28     	data cvb_HIDE_ALL
13272               	; 	' THE MESSAGE BOX, ON THE GAME SCREEN AND NOT INSTEAD OF IT.
13273               	; 	'
13274               	; 	' It used to CLS before GAME OVER, so the last thing the player saw was
13275               	; 	' the store being deleted and then two words on an empty field -- which
13276               	; 	' reads as the program ending rather than as the game ending. The store
13277               	; 	' stays; the message sits in the middle of it.
13278               	; 	'
13279               	; 	' THE BOX IS THE FONT'S OWN BACKGROUND. Every font character is black on
13280               	; 	' HUD_BG (dark blue), so a row of SPACES is a solid dark blue bar. A blank
13281               	; 	' row above and below the text is a frame for the price of two strings and
13282               	; 	' no new characters, and it reads against the store because nothing else on
13283               	; 	' the playfield is that colour.
13284               	; 	'
13285               	; 	' THIRTEEN WIDE, WHICH IS THE LONGEST MESSAGE PLUS ONE EITHER SIDE.
13286               	; 	' HE GOT AWAY and THE BIPLANE are both eleven. Every string below is padded
13287               	; 	' to exactly thirteen so the box has straight edges, and column 10 puts it
13288               	; 	' at columns 10-22 -- centre 16, the screen's.
13289               	; 	'
13290               	; 	' THE REASON IS ALWAYS ON THE SAME ROW, and that is what lets one layout
13291               	; 	' serve both cases. Losing a life draws three rows around it; losing the
13292               	; 	' last one draws two more ON TOP, so GAME OVER appears above the reason
13293               	; 	' rather than replacing it. Printing the reason at a different row for each
13294               	; 	' case would need `PRINT AT` with a variable, and every other PRINT in this
13295               	; 	' program uses a constant.
13296               	; 	PRINT AT 330,"             "

13459               	;
13460               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
13461               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
13462               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
13463               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
13464               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
13465               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
13466               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
13467               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
13468               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
13469               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
13470               	; 	' Harry was caught.

13571               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
13572               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is

13619               	; 	' A PRIZE IS AN ARPEGGIO -- testsounds PICKUP C. C5, E5, G5, then a fade
13620               	; 	' on the top note: it RISES, which is what makes it read as a reward
13621               	; 	' rather than as an event, and it ARRIVES, because a major triad resolves
13622               	; 	' where an arbitrary rise just stops.
13623               	; 	'
13624               	; 	' It was two notes struck together on two channels and held. That needed
13625               	; 	' both channels for one sound, said nothing in particular, and had no
13626               	; 	' shape at all -- a chord is a texture, and this wants to be a phrase.
13627               	; 	'
13628               	; 	' NOT THE MEASURED ONE, ON PURPOSE. The 2600 tops its pickup out at
13629               	; 	' 2543 Hz, and a square wave there is a shriek; TIA's softer timbre
13630               	; 	' carries it and the SN76489's does not. This peaks at 784 Hz. Copying the
13631               	; 	' recording would have been faithful and unpleasant, and the player earns
13632               	; 	' this sound dozens of times a round.
13633               	; 	IF sfp = 1 THEN

13803               	;
13804               	; 	' the jump sweep: divisor falling = pitch rising
13805               	; 	' THE JUMP IS A WARBLE, NOT A SWEEP -- testsounds variant A, measured off
13806               	; 	' the 2600 at about 415 Hz alternating with 188 for roughly 280 ms.
13807               	; 	'
13808               	; 	' It used to be a rising sweep, 500 down to 120 in steps of 40. That was
13809               	; 	' invented rather than measured, and it had the wrong SHAPE: a sweep reads
13810               	; 	' as something departing, which is a fine idea and is not what the original
13811               	; 	' does. Eight ticks alternating is four cycles of warble, which is what the
13812               	; 	' recording shows.
13813               	; 	'
13814               	; 	' 270 is 415 Hz and 595 is 188 Hz (divisor = 3579545 / (32 * f), so a
13815               	; 	' SMALLER divisor is a HIGHER note -- the pair reads backwards from how it
13816               	; 	' is written).
13817               	; 	IF swt > 0 THEN
13818 FC8A D020  34 	movb @cvb_SWT,r0
     FC8C 2094

13873               cv992
13874               	;
13875               	; 	' THE LOW-TIME WARNING IS SILENT. There was a beep a second under ten, and
13876               	; 	' it was the wrong instrument for the job: the last ten seconds are the
13877               	; 	' busiest part of a round, so a repeating tone arrives exactly when the
13878               	; 	' player most needs to hear the hazards, and it fought the prize arpeggio
13879               	; 	' for channel 2 as well. The flash says the same thing and says it in the
13880               	; 	' place the player is already looking.
13881               	;
13882               	; 	' FOOTSTEPS ARE NOISE, AND ON THE NOISE CHANNEL -- testsounds variant A.
13883               	; 	'
13884               	; 	' They were two alternating TONES on channel 0, which is neither what the
13885               	; 	' original does nor a good use of a channel: the recording says a footstep
13886               	; 	' is a short burst of white noise, and channel 3 was sitting unused while
13887               	; 	' the footsteps and the jump sweep shared channel 0 and had to take turns.
13888               	; 	'
13889               	; 	' TESTSOUNDS RUN D: a SHORT tick at the measured RATE.
13890               	; 	'
13891               	; 	' The two dials are independent and both were tried at the bench. The
13892               	; 	' 2600 plays a 64 ms burst every 165 ms -- six a second -- and variant A
13893               	; 	' copies both. Ten a second was tried on the way here and reads as
13894               	; 	' hurrying rather than running. What D changes is not the cadence but the
13895               	; 	' TICK: two frames rather than four, and a notch louder to carry at half
13896               	; 	' the length. Same six a second, less of it.
13897               	; 	'
13898               	; 	' EIGHT A SECOND, AND THE ACCUMULATOR NOW CARRIES ITS REMAINDER.
13899               	; 	'
13900               	; 	' sfw counted pixels and the trigger did `sfw = 0`, THROWING AWAY whatever
13901               	; 	' was past the threshold. kspd is about four pixels a pass, so a threshold
13902               	; 	' of 17 did not fire at 17 -- it fired at the first multiple past it, about
13903               	; 	' 20, and every rate this was ever set to came out slower than the number
13904               	; 	' said: the "six a second" was 5.2 and the "ten" that read as hurrying was
13905               	; 	' really 8.7. Subtracting the threshold instead keeps the fraction, so the
13906               	; 	' rate is what it claims to be at any walking speed.
13907               	; 	'
13908               	; 	' 104 px/s over 15 px is 6.9 a second -- between the 8.0 that read as
13909               	; 	' slightly hurried and the 6.1 that read as trudging. The dial is this one
13910               	; 	' constant and the subtraction below, which must stay threshold + 1 or the
13911               	; 	' carry is wrong.
13912               	; 	'
13913               	; 	' SOT = 2 IS THE SHORTEST TICK THERE IS, and 1 is SILENCE. The decay block
13914               	; 	' below runs LATER IN THE SAME sfx_tick, so a count of 1 is decremented to
13915               	; 	' zero on the pass that set it and the note-off fires before the sound has
13916               	; 	' lasted any time at all. 2 survives to the next pass, which is one pass of
13917               	; 	' sound -- about 42 ms against the bench's 33, as close as a per-pass timer
13918               	; 	' gets, and the reason the bench and the game cannot sound identical.
13919               	; 	'
13920               	; 	' Register 4 is white noise at the fastest rate.
13921               	; 	'
13922               	; 	' VOLUME 9, WHICH IS UNDER EVERYTHING ELSE ON PURPOSE. The hit, the prize
13923               	; 	' and the jump are 12 and 13, and they happen ONCE; footsteps play for the
13924               	; 	' whole round. At 13 they were level with the events they are supposed to
13925               	; 	' sit beneath, so the effects that carry information had nothing to stand
13926               	; 	' out from. The tone version was 7 for the same reason -- noise does read
13927               	; 	' quieter than a tone at the same number, which is why this is 9 and not
13928               	; 	' back to 7.
13929               	; 	IF sfw > 14 THEN
13930 FD18 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SFW,r0
     FD1A 220A

14052               	;
14053               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
14054               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
14055               	; 	'
14056               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
14057               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
14058               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
14059               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
14060               	; 	'
14061               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
14062               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
14063               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
14064               	; 	'
14065               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
14066               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
14067               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
14068               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
14069               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
14070               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
14071               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
14072               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
14073               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
14074               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
14075               	; 	#if TI994A
14076               	; 	BANK 1
14077      01E6     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

14216               	; 	' THE COLOUR TABLE, 472 bytes of the same value ($14 = black ink on dark blue).

14347               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
14348               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
14349               	; 	'
14350               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
14351               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
14352               	; 	' being small sprites.
14353               	; 	'
14354               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
14355               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
14356               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
14357               	; 	'
14358               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
14359               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
14360               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
14361               	;
14362               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
14363               cvb_SPR_KELLY
14364               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00

     63DC 0000     
     63DE 0000     
     63E0 0000

15360               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
15361               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
15362               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
15363               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
15364               	;
15365               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
15366               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
15367               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
15368               	; 	' unreadable.
15369               	;
15370               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set

15481               	; 	'   180 SLABP
15482               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
15483               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
15484               	; 	'
15485               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
15486               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
15487               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
15488               	; 	'
15489               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
15490               	; 	' blit is
15491               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
15492               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
15493               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
15494               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
15495               	; 	'
15496               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
15497               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
15498               	; 	'
15499               	; 	' template ids:
15500               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
15501               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
15502               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
15503               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
15504               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
15505               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF0
15506               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF1
15507               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF2
15508               	; 	'   8 T_ROOF3
15509               	; 	'   9 T_ROOF4
15510               	; 	'   10 T_ROOF5
15511               	; 	'   11 T_ROOF6
15512               	; 	'   12 T_ROOF7
15513               	; 	'   13 T_END_W
15514               	; 	'   14 T_END_E
15515               	;
15516               	; stor_tpl:	' 15 templates x 160 bytes
15517               cvb_STOR_TPL
15518               	; 	DATA BYTE 154,154,154,154,107,154,154,154,154,154,154,107,154,154,154,154

     72DA 9A9A
